lang=\"en\">\n<h1 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_document\">\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id3.id1\">Mem0</span>: Building Production<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_align_center\" id=\"id4.id2\">-</span>Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_align_center\" id=\"id5.id3\">-</span>Term Memory</h1><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_authors\">\n<span class=\"ltx_creator ltx_role_author\">\n<span class=\"ltx_personname\">Prateek Chhikara\n</span><span class=\"ltx_break\">\n&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; <a class=\"ltx_ref ltx_href ltx_font_typewriter\" href=\"mailto:research@mem0.ai\" title=\"\">research@mem0.ai</a>\n</span><span class=\"ltx_author_notes\">\n<span class=\"ltx_contact ltx_role_affiliation\">\n</span></span></span>\n</div><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_abstract\" id=\"abstract\">\n<h6 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_abstract\">Abstract</h6><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"id1.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"id1.1.1\">Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable prowess in generating contextually coherent responses, yet their fixed context windows pose fundamental challenges for maintaining consistency over prolonged multi-session dialogues. We introduce <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id1.1.1.1\">Mem0</span>, a scalable memory-centric architecture that addresses this issue by dynamically extracting, consolidating, and retrieving salient information from ongoing conversations. Building on this foundation, we further propose an enhanced variant that leverages graph-based memory representations to capture complex relational structures among conversational elements.\nThrough comprehensive evaluations on the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id1.1.1.2\">LOCOMO</span> benchmark, we systematically compare our approaches against six baseline categories: (i) established memory-augmented systems, (ii) retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) with varying chunk sizes and\n<math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"id1.1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"id1.1.1.m1.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"id1.1.1.m1.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math>-values, (iii) a full-context approach that processes the entire conversation history, (iv) an open-source memory solution, (v) a proprietary model system, and (vi) a dedicated memory management platform.\nEmpirical results demonstrate that our methods consistently outperform all existing memory systems across four question categories: single-hop, temporal, multi-hop, and open-domain.\nNotably, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id1.1.1.3\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;achieves 26% relative improvements in the LLM-as-a-Judge metric over OpenAI, while <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id1.1.1.4\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;with graph memory achieves around 2% higher overall score than the base <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id1.1.1.5\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;configuration.\nBeyond accuracy gains, we also markedly reduce computational overhead compared to the full-context approach. In particular, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"id1.1.1.6\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;attains a 91% lower p95 latency and saves more than 90% token cost, thereby offering a compelling balance between advanced reasoning capabilities and practical deployment constraints.\nOur findings highlight the critical role of structured, persistent memory mechanisms for long-term conversational coherence, paving the way for more reliable and efficient LLM-driven AI agents.</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"id7.id1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"id7.id1.1\">Code can be found at<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_medium\" id=\"id7.id1.1.1\">: <a class=\"ltx_ref ltx_href\" href=\"https://mem0.ai/research\" title=\"\">https://mem0.ai/research</a></span></span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"id2\">\n<img alt=\"[Uncaptioned image]\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"16\" id=\"id2.g1\" src=\"extracted/6393986/figures/mem0_logo_bw_long.png\" width=\"60\">\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S1\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">1 </span>Introduction</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p1.1\">Human memory is a <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S1.p1.1.1\">foundation of intelligence</em>—it shapes our identity, guides decision-making, and enables us to learn, adapt, and form meaningful relationships <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Craik and Jennings, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib5\" title=\"\">1992</a>)</cite>. Among its many roles, memory is essential for communication: we recall past interactions, infer preferences, and construct evolving mental models of those we engage with <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Assmann, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib2\" title=\"\">2011</a>)</cite>. This ability to retain and retrieve information over extended periods enables coherent, contextually rich exchanges that span days, weeks, or even months. AI agents, powered by large language models (LLMs), have made remarkable progress in generating fluent, contextually appropriate responses <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Yu et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib26\" title=\"\">2024</a>, Zhang et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib29\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>. However, these systems are fundamentally limited by their reliance on fixed context windows, which severely restrict their ability to maintain coherence over extended interactions <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Bulatov et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib3\" title=\"\">2022</a>, Liu et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib13\" title=\"\">2023</a>)</cite>.\nThis limitation stems from LLMs’ lack of persistent memory mechanisms that can extend beyond their finite context windows. While humans naturally accumulate and organize experiences over time, forming a continuous narrative of interactions, AI systems cannot inherently persist information across separate sessions or after context overflow.\nThe absence of persistent memory creates a fundamental disconnect in human-AI interaction. Without memory, AI agents forget user preferences, repeat questions, and contradict previously established facts.\nConsider a simple example illustrated in Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S1.F1\" title=\"Figure 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">1</span></a>, where a user mentions being vegetarian and avoiding dairy products in an initial conversation.\nIn a subsequent session, when the user asks about dinner recommendations, a system without persistent memory might suggest chicken, completely contradicting the established dietary preferences. In contrast, a system with persistent memory would maintain this critical user information across sessions and suggest appropriate vegetarian, dairy-free options. This common scenario highlights how memory failures can fundamentally undermine user experience and trust.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p2.1\">Beyond conversational settings, memory mechanisms have been shown to dramatically enhance agent performance in interactive environments <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib15\" title=\"\">Majumder et&nbsp;al., </a>, Shinn et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib20\" title=\"\">2023</a>)</cite>. Agents equipped with memory of past experiences can better anticipate user needs, learn from previous mistakes, and generalize knowledge across tasks <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Chhikara et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib4\" title=\"\">2023</a>)</cite>. Research demonstrates that memory-augmented agents improve decision-making by leveraging causal relationships between actions and outcomes, leading to more effective adaptation in dynamic scenarios <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Rasmussen et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib18\" title=\"\">2025</a>)</cite>. Hierarchical memory architectures <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Packer et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib17\" title=\"\">2023</a>, Sarthi et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib19\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite> and agentic memory systems capable of autonomous evolution <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Xu et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib25\" title=\"\">2025</a>)</cite> have further shown that memory enables more coherent, long-term reasoning across multiple dialogue sessions.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_figure ltx_figure_panel ltx_minipage ltx_align_center ltx_align_top\" id=\"S1.F1\" style=\"width:138.8pt;\">\n<div class=\"ltx_block ltx_figure_panel ltx_minipage ltx_align_top\" id=\"S1.1.1\" style=\"width:281.9pt;\">\n<img alt=\"Refer to caption\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"291\" id=\"S1.1.1.g1\" src=\"extracted/6393986/figures/main_figure.png\" width=\"598\">\n</div>\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure\"><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S1.F1.6.1.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Figure 1</span>: </span><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S1.F1.7.2\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Illustration of memory importance in AI agents.<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_medium\" id=\"S1.F1.7.2.1\">\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S1.F1.7.2.1.1\">Left</span>: Without persistent memory, the system forgets critical user information (vegetarian, dairy-free preferences) between sessions, resulting in inappropriate recommendations.\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S1.F1.7.2.1.2\">Right</span>: With effective memory, the system maintains these dietary preferences across interactions, enabling contextually appropriate suggestions that align with previously established constraints.</span></span></figcaption>\n</figure><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p3.1\">Unlike humans, who dynamically integrate new information and revise outdated beliefs, LLMs effectively “<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S1.p3.1.1\">reset</span>\" once information falls outside their context window <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Zhang, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib27\" title=\"\">2024</a>, Timoneda and Vera, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib24\" title=\"\">2025</a>)</cite>.\nEven as models like OpenAI’s GPT-4 (128K tokens) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Hurst et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib10\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>, o1 (200K context) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Jaech et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib11\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>, Anthropic’s Claude 3.7 Sonnet (200K tokens) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Anthropic, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib1\" title=\"\">2025</a>)</cite>, and Google’s Gemini (at least 10M tokens) <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Team et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib23\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite> push the boundaries of context length, these improvements merely delay rather than solve the fundamental limitation.\nIn practical applications, even these extended context windows prove insufficient for two critical reasons. First, as meaningful human-AI relationships develop over weeks or months, conversation history inevitably exceeds even the most generous context limits. Second, and perhaps more importantly, real-world conversations rarely maintain thematic continuity. A user might mention dietary preferences (being vegetarian), then engage in hours of unrelated discussion about programming tasks, before returning to food-related queries about dinner options. In such scenarios, a full-context approach would need to reason through mountains of irrelevant information, with the critical dietary preferences potentially buried among thousands of tokens of coding discussions. Moreover, simply presenting longer contexts does not ensure effective retrieval or utilization of past information, as attention mechanisms degrade over distant tokens <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Guo et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib7\" title=\"\">2024</a>, Nelson et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib16\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>.\nThis limitation is particularly problematic in high-stakes domains such as healthcare, education, and enterprise support, where maintaining continuity and trust is crucial <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Hatalis et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib8\" title=\"\">2023</a>)</cite>. To address these challenges, AI agents must adopt memory systems that go beyond static context extension. A robust AI memory should selectively store important information, consolidate related concepts, and retrieve relevant details when needed—<em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S1.p3.1.2\">mirroring human cognitive processes</em> <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(He et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib9\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>. By integrating such mechanisms, we can develop AI agents that maintain consistent personas, track evolving user preferences, and build upon prior exchanges. This shift will transform AI from transient, forgetful responders into reliable, long-term collaborators, fundamentally redefining the future of conversational intelligence.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S1.p4\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S1.p4.1\">In this paper, we address a fundamental limitation in AI systems: their inability to maintain coherent reasoning across extended conversations across different sessions, which severely restricts meaningful long-term interactions with users. We introduce <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S1.p4.1.1\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;(pronounced as <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S1.p4.1.2\">mem-zero</em>), a novel memory architecture that dynamically captures, organizes, and retrieves salient information from ongoing conversations. Building on this foundation, we develop <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S1.p4.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>, which enhances the base architecture with graph-based memory representations to better model complex relationships between conversational elements.\nOur experimental results on the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S1.p4.1.3\">LOCOMO</span> benchmark demonstrate that our approaches consistently outperform existing memory systems—including memory-augmented architectures, retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods, and both open-source and proprietary solutions—across diverse question types, while simultaneously requiring significantly lower computational resources.\nLatency measurements further reveal that <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S1.p4.1.4\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;operates with 91% lower response times than full-context approaches, striking an optimal balance between sophisticated reasoning capabilities and practical deployment constraints. These contributions represent a meaningful step toward AI systems that can maintain coherent, context-aware conversations over extended durations—mirroring human communication patterns and opening new possibilities for applications in personal tutoring, healthcare, and personalized assistance.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S2\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">2 </span>Proposed Methods</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.p1.2\">We introduce two memory architectures for AI agents. <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S2.p1.2.1\">(1)</span> <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.p1.2.2\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;implements a novel paradigm that extracts, evaluates, and manages salient information from conversations through dedicated modules for memory extraction and updation. The system processes a pair of messages between either two user participants or a user and an assistant. <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S2.p1.2.3\">(2)</span> <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.p1.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;extends this foundation by incorporating graph-based memory representations, where memories are stored as directed labeled graphs with entities as nodes and relationships as edges. This structure enables a deeper understanding of the connections between entities. By explicitly modeling both entities and their relationships, <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1a\"><msup id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.p1.2.m2.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;supports more advanced reasoning across interconnected facts, especially for queries that require navigating complex relational paths across multiple memories.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S2.SS1\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">2.1 </span><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS1.1.1\">Mem0</span>\n</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS1.p1.1\">Our architecture follows an incremental processing paradigm, enabling it to operate seamlessly within ongoing conversations. As illustrated in Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S2.F2\" title=\"Figure 2 ‣ 2.1 Mem0 ‣ 2 Proposed Methods ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">2</span></a>, the complete pipeline architecture consists of two phases: <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S2.SS1.p1.1.1\">extraction</span> and <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S2.SS1.p1.1.2\">update</span>.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_figure\" id=\"S2.F2\"><img alt=\"Refer to caption\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"250\" id=\"S2.F2.g1\" src=\"extracted/6393986/figures/mem0_pipeline.png\" width=\"598\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure\"><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S2.F2.3.1.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Figure 2</span>: </span><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S2.F2.4.2\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Architectural overview of the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.F2.4.2.1\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;system showing extraction and update phase. The extraction phase processes messages and historical context to create new memories. The update phase evaluates these extracted memories against similar existing ones, applying appropriate operations through a Tool Call mechanism. The database serves as the central repository, providing context for processing and storing updated memories.</span></figcaption>\n</figure><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.11\">The <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.11.1\">extraction phase</span> initiates upon ingestion of a new message pair <math alttext=\"(m_{t-1},m_{t})\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.1.m1.2\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS1.p2.6.m6.1d\">italic_m</annotation></semantics></math> is a hyperparameter controlling the recency window. To support context-aware memory extraction, we implement an asynchronous summary generation module that periodically refreshes the conversation summary. This component operates independently of the main processing pipeline, ensuring that memory extraction consistently benefits from up-to-date contextual information without introducing processing delays. While <math alttext=\"S\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1a\"><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1.1.cmml\">S</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1b\"><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1.1\">𝑆</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1c\">S</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS1.p2.7.m7.1d\">italic_S</annotation></semantics></math> provides global thematic understanding across the entire conversation, the recent message sequence offers granular temporal context that may contain relevant details not consolidated in the summary. id=\"S2.SS1.p2.11.m11.2.2.1.1.1.3.cmml\" type=\"integer\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p2.11.m11.2.2.1.1.1.3\">1</cn></apply><apply id=\"S2.SS1.p2.11.m11.3.3.2.2.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p2.11.m11.3.3.2.2.2\"><csymbol start_POSTSUBSCRIPT 1 end_POSTSUBSCRIPT , italic_ω start_POSTSUBSCRIPT 2 end_POSTSUBSCRIPT , … , italic_ω start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_n end_POSTSUBSCRIPT }</annotation></semantics></math> specifically from the new exchange while maintaining awareness of the conversation’s broader context, resulting in candidate facts for potential inclusion in the knowledge base.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2\">Following extraction, the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.1\">update phase</span> evaluates each candidate fact against existing memories to maintain consistency and avoid redundancy. This phase determines the appropriate memory management operation for each extracted fact <math alttext=\"\\omega_{i}\\in\\Omega\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1a\"><mrow id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><msub id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\"><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.2\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.2.cmml\">ω</mi><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.3\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.3.cmml\">i</mi></msub><mo id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\">∈</mo><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathvariant=\"normal\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">Ω</mi></mrow><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1\"><in id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.1\"></in><apply id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2\">subscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.2\">𝜔</ci><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.2.3\">𝑖</ci></apply><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1.1.3\">Ω</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1c\">\\omega_{i}\\in\\Omega</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.1.m1.1d\">italic_ω start_POSTSUBSCRIPT italic_i end_POSTSUBSCRIPT ∈ roman_Ω</annotation></semantics></math>. Algorithm <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#alg1\" title=\"Algorithm 1 ‣ Appendix B Algorithm ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">1</span></a>, mentioned in Appendix <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#A2\" title=\"Appendix B Algorithm ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">B</span></a>, illustrates this process. For each fact, the system first retrieves the top <math alttext=\"s\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1a\"><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1.1.cmml\">s</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1.1\">𝑠</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1c\">s</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.m2.1d\">italic_s</annotation></semantics></math> semantically similar memories using vector embeddings from the database. These retrieved memories, along with the candidate fact, are then presented to the LLM through a function-calling interface we refer to as a ‘tool call.’ The LLM itself determines which of four distinct operations to execute: <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.2\">ADD</span> for creation of new memories when no semantically equivalent memory exists; <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.3\">UPDATE</span> for augmentation of existing memories with complementary information; <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.4\">DELETE</span> for removal of memories contradicted by new information; and <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS1.p3.2.5\">NOOP</span> when the candidate fact requires no modification to the knowledge base. Rather than using a separate classifier, we leverage the LLM’s reasoning capabilities to directly select the appropriate operation based on the semantic relationship between the candidate fact and existing memories. Following this determination, the system executes the provided operations, thereby maintaining knowledge base coherence and temporal consistency.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2\">In our experimental evaluation, we configured the system with ‘<math alttext=\"m\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">m</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1.1\">𝑚</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1c\">m</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.1.m1.1d\">italic_m</annotation></semantics></math>’ = 10 previous messages for contextual reference and ‘<math alttext=\"s\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1a\"><mi id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1.1.cmml\">s</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1.1\">𝑠</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1c\">s</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.m2.1d\">italic_s</annotation></semantics></math>’ = 10 similar memories for comparative analysis. All language model operations utilized <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS1.p4.2.1\">GPT-4o-mini</span> as the inference engine. The vector database employs dense embeddings to facilitate efficient similarity search during the update phase.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S2.SS2\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">2.2 </span><math class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_figure\" id=\"S2.F3\"><img alt=\"Refer to caption\" class=\"ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape\" height=\"182\" id=\"S2.F3.g1\" src=\"extracted/6393986/figures/mem0p_pipeline.png\" width=\"598\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure\"><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S2.F3.4.2.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Figure 3</span>: </span><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Graph-based memory architecture of <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1b\"><msup id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1.2\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1c\"><apply id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1d\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.F3.2.1.m1.1e\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;illustrating entity extraction and update phase. The extraction phase uses LLMs to convert conversation messages into entities and relation triplets. The update phase employs conflict detection and resolution mechanisms when integrating new information into the existing knowledge graph.</span></figcaption>\n</figure><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS2.p4\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS2.p4.1\">The extraction process employs a two-stage pipeline leveraging LLMs to transform unstructured text into structured graph representations. First, an <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S2.SS2.p4.1.1\">entity extractor</span> module processes the input text to identify a set of entities along with their corresponding types. In our framework, entities represent the key information elements in conversations—including people, locations, objects, concepts, events, and attributes that merit representation in the memory graph. The entity extractor identifies these diverse information units by analyzing the semantic importance, uniqueness, and persistence of elements in the conversation. For instance, in a conversation about travel plans, entities might include destinations (cities, countries), transportation modes, dates, activities, and participant preferences—essentially any discrete information that could be relevant for future reference or reasoning.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2\">Next, a <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.1\">relationship generator</span> component derives meaningful connections between these entities, establishing a set of relationship triplets that capture the semantic structure of the information. This LLM-based module analyzes the extracted entities and their context within the conversation to identify semantically significant connections. It works by examining linguistic patterns, contextual cues, and domain knowledge to determine how entities relate to one another. For each potential entity pair, the generator evaluates whether a meaningful relationship exists and, if so, classifies this relationship with an appropriate label (e.g., ‘lives_in’, ‘prefers’, ‘owns’, ‘happened_on’). The module employs prompt engineering techniques that guide the LLM to reason about both explicit statements and implicit information in the dialogue, resulting in relationship triplets that form the edges in our memory graph and enable complex reasoning across interconnected information.\nWhen integrating new information, <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;employs a sophisticated storage and update strategy. For each new relationship triple, we compute embeddings for both source and destination entities, then search for existing nodes with semantic similarity above a defined threshold ‘<math alttext=\"t\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1a\"><mi id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1.1.cmml\">t</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1.1\">𝑡</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1c\">t</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.m2.1d\">italic_t</annotation></semantics></math>’. Based on node existence, the system may create both nodes, create only one node, or use existing nodes before establishing the relationship with appropriate metadata. To maintain a consistent knowledge graph, we implement a <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.2\">conflict detection</span> mechanism that identifies potentially conflicting existing relationships when new information arrives. An LLM-based <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S2.SS2.p5.2.3\">update resolver</span> determines if certain relationships should be obsolete, marking them as invalid rather than physically removing them to enable temporal reasoning.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2\">The memory retrieval functionality in <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;implements a dual-approach strategy for optimal information access. The entity-centric method first identifies key entities within a query, then leverages semantic similarity to locate corresponding nodes in the knowledge graph. It systematically explores both incoming and outgoing relationships from these anchor nodes, constructing a comprehensive subgraph that captures relevant contextual information. Complementing this, the semantic triplet approach takes a more holistic view by encoding the entire query as a dense embedding vector. This query representation is then matched against textual encodings of each relationship triplet in the knowledge graph. The system calculates fine-grained similarity scores between the query and all available triplets, returning only those that exceed a configurable relevance threshold, ranked in order of decreasing similarity. This dual retrieval mechanism enables <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1a\"><msup id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS2.p6.2.m2.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;to handle both targeted entity-focused questions and broader conceptual queries with equal effectiveness.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1\">From an implementation perspective, the system utilizes Neo4j<span class=\"ltx_note ltx_role_footnote\" id=\"footnote1\"><sup class=\"ltx_note_mark\">1</sup><span class=\"ltx_note_outer\"><span class=\"ltx_note_content\"><sup class=\"ltx_note_mark\">1</sup><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_note\">1</span><a class=\"ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter\" href=\"https://neo4j.com/\" title=\"\">https://neo4j.com/</a></span></span></span> as the underlying graph database. LLM-based extractors and update module leverage <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.1\">GPT-4o-mini</span> with function calling capabilities, allowing for structured extraction of information from unstructured text. By combining graph-based representations with semantic embeddings and LLM-based information extraction, <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S2.SS2.p7.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;achieves both the structural richness needed for complex reasoning and the semantic flexibility required for natural language understanding.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S3\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">3 </span>Experimental Setup</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S3.SS1\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">3.1 </span>Dataset</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS1.p1.1\">The <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS1.p1.1.1\">LOCOMO</span> <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Maharana et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib14\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite> dataset is designed to evaluate long-term conversational memory in dialogue systems. It comprises 10 extended conversations, each containing approximately 600 dialogues and 26000 tokens on average, distributed across multiple sessions. Each conversation captures two individuals discussing daily experiences or past events. Following these multi-session dialogues, each conversation is accompanied by 200 questions on an average with corresponding ground truth answers. These questions are categorized into multiple types: single-hop, multi-hop, temporal, and open-domain. The dataset originally included an adversarial question category, which was designed to test systems’ ability to recognize unanswerable questions. However, this category was excluded from our evaluation because ground truth answers were unavailable, and the expected behavior for this question type is that the agent should recognize them as unanswerable.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S3.SS2\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">3.2 </span>Evaluation Metrics</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS2.p1.1\">Our evaluation framework implements a comprehensive approach to assess long-term memory capabilities in dialogue systems, considering both response quality and operational efficiency. We categorize our metrics into two distinct groups that together provide a holistic understanding of system performance.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.1.1\">(1)</span> Performance Metrics</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1\">Previous research in conversational AI <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Goswami, <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib6\" title=\"\">2025</a>, Soni et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib22\" title=\"\">2024</a>, Singh et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib21\" title=\"\">2020</a>)</cite> has predominantly relied on lexical similarity metrics such as <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1\">F1 Score</span> (F<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2\">1</sub>) and <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3\">BLEU-1</span> (B<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.4\">1</sub>). However, these metrics exhibit significant limitations when evaluating factual accuracy in conversational contexts. Consider a scenario where the ground truth answer is ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.5\">Alice was born in March</span>’ and a system generates ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.6\">Alice is born in July</span>.’ Despite containing a critical factual error regarding the birth month, traditional metrics would assign relatively high scores due to lexical overlap in the remaining tokens (‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.7\">Alice</span>,’ ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.8\">born</span>,’ etc.). This fundamental limitation can lead to misleading evaluations that fail to capture semantic correctness.\nTo address these shortcomings, we use <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.9\">LLM-as-a-Judge</span> (J) as a complementary evaluation metric. This approach leverages a separate, more capable LLM to assess response quality across multiple dimensions, including factual accuracy, relevance, completeness, and contextual appropriateness. The judge model analyzes the question, ground truth answer and the generated answer, providing a more nuanced evaluation that aligns better with human judgment. Due to the stochastic nature of J&nbsp;evaluations, we conducted 10 independent runs for each method on the entire dataset and report the mean scores along with <math alttext=\"\\pm\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mo id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">±</mo><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1b\"><csymbol cd=\"latexml\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1\">plus-or-minus</csymbol></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\pm</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1d\">±</annotation></semantics></math>1 standard deviation. More details about the J&nbsp;is present in Appendix <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#A1\" title=\"Appendix A Prompts ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">A</span></a>.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.1.1\">(2)</span> Deployment Metrics</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1\">Beyond response quality, practical deployment considerations are crucial for real-world applications of long-term memory in AI agents. We systematically track <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1\">Token Consumption</span>, using ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2\">cl100k_base</span>’ encoding from <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.3\">tiktoken</span>, measuring the number of tokens extracted during retrieval that serve as context for answering queries. For our memory-based models, these tokens represent the memories retrieved from the knowledge base, while for RAG-based models, they correspond to the total number of tokens in the retrieved text chunks. This distinction is important as it directly affects operational costs and system efficiency—whether processing concise memory facts or larger raw text segments. We further monitor <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.4\">Latency</span>, (i) <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.5\">search latency</em>: which captures the total time required to search the memory (in memory-based solutions) or chunk (in RAG-based solutions) and (ii) <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.6\">total latency:</em> time to generate appropriate responses, consisting of both retrieval time (accessing memories or chunks) and answer generation time using the LLM.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p2.1\">The relationship between these metrics reveals important trade-offs in system design. For instance, more sophisticated memory architectures might achieve higher factual accuracy but at the cost of increased token consumption and latency. Our multi-dimensional evaluation methodology enables researchers and practitioners to make informed decisions based on their specific requirements, whether prioritizing response quality for critical applications or computational efficiency for real-time deployment scenarios.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S3.SS3\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">3.3 </span>Baselines</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.p1.1\">To comprehensively evaluate our approach, we compare against six distinct categories of baselines that represent the current state of conversational memory systems. These diverse baselines collectively provide a robust framework for evaluating the effectiveness of different memory architectures across various dimensions, including factual accuracy, computational efficiency, and scalability to extended conversations. Where applicable, unless otherwise specified, we set the temperature to 0 to ensure the runs are as reproducible as possible.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Established <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.1.1\">LOCOMO</span> Benchmarks</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1\">We first establish a comparative foundation by evaluating previously benchmarked methods on the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1\">LOCOMO</span> dataset. These include five established approaches: LoCoMo <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Maharana et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib14\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>, ReadAgent <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Lee et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib12\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>, MemoryBank <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Zhong et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib30\" title=\"\">2024</a>)</cite>, MemGPT <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Packer et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib17\" title=\"\">2023</a>)</cite>, and A-Mem <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Xu et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib25\" title=\"\">2025</a>)</cite>. These established benchmarks not only provide direct comparison points with published results but also represent the evolution of conversational memory architectures across different algorithmic paradigms. For our evaluation, we select the metrics where <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2\">gpt-4o-mini</span> was used for the evaluation. More details about these benchmarks are mentioned in Appendix <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#A3\" title=\"Appendix C Selected Baselines ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">C</span></a>.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Open-Source Memory Solutions</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1.1\">Our second category consists of promising open-source memory architectures such as LangMem<span class=\"ltx_note ltx_role_footnote\" id=\"footnote2\"><sup class=\"ltx_note_mark\">2</sup><span class=\"ltx_note_outer\"><span class=\"ltx_note_content\"><sup class=\"ltx_note_mark\">2</sup><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_note\">2</span><a class=\"ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter\" href=\"https://langchain-ai.github.io/langmem/\" title=\"\">https://langchain-ai.github.io/langmem/</a></span></span></span> (Hot Path) that have demonstrated effectiveness in related conversational tasks but have not yet been evaluated on the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1\">LOCOMO</span> dataset. By adapting these systems to our evaluation framework, we broaden the comparative landscape and identify potential alternative approaches that may offer competitive performance. We initialized the LLM with <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2\">gpt-4o-mini</span> and used <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.3\">text-embedding-small-3</span> as the embedding model.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7\">As a baseline, we treat the entire conversation history as a document collection and apply a standard RAG pipeline. We first segment each conversation into fixed-length chunks (128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, and 8192 tokens), where 8192 is the maximum chunk size supported by our embedding model. All chunks are embedded using OpenAI’s <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.1\">text-embedding-small-3</span> to ensure consistent vector quality across configurations. At query time, we retrieve the top <math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1a\"><mi id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.m1.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math> chunks by semantic similarity and concatenate them as context for answer generation. Throughout our experiments we set <math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1a\"><mi id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.2.m2.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math><math alttext=\"\\in\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1a\"><mo id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\">∈</mo><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1b\"><in id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1.1\"></in></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1c\">\\in</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.3.m3.1d\">∈</annotation></semantics></math>{1,2}: with <math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1a\"><mi id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.4.m4.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math>=1 only the single most relevant chunk is used, and with <math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1a\"><mi id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.5.m5.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math>=2 the two most relevant chunks (up to 16384 tokens) are concatenated. We avoid <math alttext=\"k&gt;2\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1a\"><mrow id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.cmml\"><mi id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.2\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.2.cmml\">k</mi><mo id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.1.cmml\">&gt;</mo><mn id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.3\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.3.cmml\">2</mn></mrow><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1b\"><apply id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1\"><gt id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.1\"></gt><ci id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.2\">𝑘</ci><cn id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.3.cmml\" type=\"integer\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1.1.3\">2</cn></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1c\">k&gt;2</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.6.m6.1d\">italic_k &gt; 2</annotation></semantics></math> since the average conversation length (26000 tokens) would be fully covered, negating the benefits of selective retrieval. By varying chunk size and <math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1\"><semantics id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1a\"><mi id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1.1\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1b\"><ci id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1.7.m7.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math>, we systematically evaluate RAG performance on long-term conversational memory tasks.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px4\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Full-Context Processing</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px4.p1.1\">We adopt a straightforward approach by passing the entire conversation history within the context window of the LLM. This method leverages the model’s inherent ability to process sequential information without additional architectural components. While conceptually simple, this approach faces practical limitations as conversation length increases, eventually increasing token cost and latency. Nevertheless, it establishes an important reference point for understanding the value of more sophisticated memory mechanisms compared to direct processing of available context.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px5\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Proprietary Models</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px5.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px5.p1.1\">We evaluate OpenAI’s memory<span class=\"ltx_note ltx_role_footnote\" id=\"footnote3\"><sup class=\"ltx_note_mark\">3</sup><span class=\"ltx_note_outer\"><span class=\"ltx_note_content\"><sup class=\"ltx_note_mark\">3</sup><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_note\">3</span><a class=\"ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter\" href=\"https://openai.com/index/memory-and-new-controls-for-chatgpt/\" title=\"\">https://openai.com/index/memory-and-new-controls-for-chatgpt/</a></span></span></span> feature available in their ChatGPT interface, specifically using <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px5.p1.1.1\">gpt-4o-mini</span> for consistency. We ingest entire <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px5.p1.1.2\">LOCOMO</span> conversations with a prompt (see Appendix <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#A1\" title=\"Appendix A Prompts ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">A</span></a>) into single chat sessions, prompting memory generation with timestamps, participant names, and conversation text. These generated memories are then used as complete context for answering questions about each conversation, intentionally granting the OpenAI approach privileged access to all memories rather than only question-relevant ones. This methodology accommodates the lack of external API access for selective memory retrieval in OpenAI’s system for benchmarking.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px6\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Memory Providers</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px6.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px6.p1.1\">We incorporate Zep <cite class=\"ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep\">(Rasmussen et&nbsp;al., <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#bib.bib18\" title=\"\">2025</a>)</cite>, a memory management platform designed for AI agents. Using their platform version, we conduct systematic evaluations across the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.SS3.SSS0.Px6.p1.1.1\">LOCOMO</span> dataset, maintaining temporal fidelity by preserving timestamp information alongside conversational content. This temporal anchoring ensures that time-sensitive queries can be addressed through appropriately contextualized memory retrieval, particularly important for evaluating questions that require chronological awareness. This baseline represents an important commercial implementation of memory management specifically engineered for AI agents.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_table\" id=\"S3.T1\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_table\"><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S3.T1.25.4.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Table 1</span>: </span><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S3.T1.6.3\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Performance comparison of memory-enabled systems across different question types in the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S3.T1.6.3.1\">LOCOMO</span> dataset. element</button>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S4.SS1\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">4.1 </span>Performance Comparison Across Memory-Enabled Systems</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1\">Table&nbsp;<a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S3.T1\" title=\"Table 1 ‣ Memory Providers ‣ 3.3 Baselines ‣ 3 Experimental Setup ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">1</span></a> reports F<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.1\">1</sub>, B<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.2\">1</sub>&nbsp;and J&nbsp;scores for our two architectures—<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.3\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;and <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1\" cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;—against a suite of competitive baselines, as mentioned in Section <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S3\" title=\"3 Experimental Setup ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">3</span></a>, on single<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.4\">-</span>hop, multi<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.5\">-</span>hop, open<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.6\">-</span>domain, and temporal questions. Overall, both of our models set new state<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.7\">-</span>of<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.8\">-</span>the<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.p1.1.9\">-</span>art marks in all the three evaluation metrics for most question types.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Single-Hop Question Performance</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1\">Single<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1\">-</span>hop queries involve locating a single factual span contained within one dialogue turn. Leveraging its dense memories in natural language text, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;secures the strongest results:F<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3\">1</sub>=38.72, B<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.4\">1</sub>=27.13, and J=67.13. Augmenting the natural language memories with graph memory (<math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>) yields marginal performance drop compared to <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.5\">Mem0</span>, indicating that relational structure provides limited utility when the retrieval target occupies a single turn. Among the existing baselines, the full<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.6\">-</span>context <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.7\">OpenAI</span> run attains the next<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.8\">-</span>best J&nbsp;score, reflecting the benefits of retaining the entire conversation in context, while LangMem and Zep both score around 8% relatively less against our models on J&nbsp;score. Previous <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.9\">LOCOMO</span> benchmarks such as A-mem lag by more than 25 points in J, underscoring the necessity of fine<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.10\">-</span>grained, structured memory indexing even for simple retrieval tasks.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px2\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Multi-Hop Question Performance</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.1\">Multi-hop queries require synthesizing information dispersed across multiple conversation sessions, posing significant challenges in memory integration and retrieval. <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;clearly outperforms other methods with an F<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2\">1</sub>&nbsp;score of 28.64 and a J&nbsp;score of 51.15, reflecting its capability to efficiently retrieve and integrate disparate information stored across sessions.
