class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">Temporal Reasoning Performance</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1\">Temporal reasoning tasks hinge on accurate modeling of event sequences, their relative ordering, and durations within conversational history. Our architectures demonstrate substantial improvements across all metrics, with <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;achieving the highest F<sub class=\"ltx_sub\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.1\">1</sub>(51.55) and J&nbsp;(58.13), suggesting that structured relational representations in addition to natural language memories significantly aid in temporally grounded judgments. Notably, the base variant, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.2\">Mem0</span>, also provide a decent J&nbsp;score (55.51), suggesting that natural language alone can aid in temporally grounded judgments.\nAmong baselines, OpenAI notably underperforms, with scores below 15%, primarily due to missing timestamps in most generated memories despite explicit prompting in the OpenAI ChatGPT to extract memories with timestamps. Other baselines such as A-Mem achieve respectable results, yet our models clearly advance the state-of-the-art, emphasizing the critical advantage of accurately leveraging both natural language contextualization and structured graph representations for temporal reasoning.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S4.SS2\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">4.2 Although graph-based representations in <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.2.m2.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;slightly lag behind in lexical overlap metrics for these simpler queries, they significantly enhance semantic coherence, as demonstrated by competitive J&nbsp;scores. This indicates that graph structures are more beneficial in scenarios involving nuanced relational context rather than straightforward retrieval. For multi-hop questions, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.3\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;exhibits clear advantages by effectively synthesizing dispersed information across multiple sessions, confirming that natural language memories provide sufficient representational richness for these integrative tasks. Surprisingly, the expected relational advantages of <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS2.p1.3.m3.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;do not translate into better outcomes here, suggesting potential overhead or redundancy when navigating more intricate graph structures in multi-step reasoning scenarios.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<figure class=\"ltx_table\" id=\"S4.T2\">\n<figcaption class=\"ltx_caption ltx_centering\"><span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_table\"><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.T2.28.2.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Table 2</span>: </span><span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.T2.2.1\" style=\"font-size:90%;\">Performance comparison of various baselines with proposed methods. These advances underscore the advantage of capturing only the most salient facts in memory, rather than retrieving large chunk of original text. By converting the conversation history into concise, structured representations, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.3\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;and <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS3.p1.5.m5.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;mitigate noise and surface more precise cues to the LLM, leading to better answers as evaluated by an external LLM (J).</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS3.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS3.p2.3\">Despite these improvements, a full-context method that ingests a chunk of roughly 26,000 tokens still achieves the highest J&nbsp;score (approximately 73%). However, as shown in Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S4.F4.sf2\" title=\"Figure 4(b) ‣ Figure 4 ‣ 4.2 Cross-Category Analysis ‣ 4 Evaluation Results, Analysis and Discussion. ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">4(b)</span></a>, it also incurs a very high total p95 latency—around 17 seconds—since the model must read the entire conversation on every query. Although the full-context approach can provide a slight accuracy edge, the memory-based systems offer a more practical trade-off, maintaining near-competitive quality while imposing only a fraction of the token and latency cost.\nAs conversation length increases, full-context approaches suffer from exponential growth in computational overhead (evident in Table <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S4.T2\" title=\"Table 2 ‣ 4.2 Cross-Category Analysis ‣ 4 Evaluation Results, Analysis and Discussion. ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">2</span></a> where total p95 latency increases significantly with larger <math alttext=\"k\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1a\"><mi id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1.1.cmml\">k</mi><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1b\"><ci id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1.1\">𝑘</ci></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1c\">k</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS3.p2.2.m2.1d\">italic_k</annotation></semantics></math> values or chunk sizes). This increase in input chunks leads to longer response times and higher token consumption costs. class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S4.SS4\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">4.4 </span>Latency Analysis</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS4.p1.1\">Table <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S4.T2\" title=\"Table 2 ‣ 4.2 Cross-Category Analysis ‣ 4 Evaluation Results, Analysis and Discussion. ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">2</span></a> provides a comprehensive performance comparison of various retrieval and memory methodologies, presenting median (p50) and tail (p95) latencies for both the search phase and total response generation across the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS4.p1.1.1\">LOCOMO</span> dataset. Our analysis reveals distinct performance patterns governed by architectural choices. Memory-centric architectures demonstrate different performance characteristics. A-Mem, despite its larger memory store, incurs substantial search overhead (p50: 0.668s), resulting in total median latencies of 1.410s. LangMem exhibits even higher search latencies (p50: 17.99s, p95: 59.82s), rendering it impractical for interactive applications. Zep achieves moderate performance (p50 total: 1.292s).\nThe full-context baseline, which processes the entire conversation history without retrieval, fundamentally differs from retrieval-based approaches. By passing the entire conversation context (26000 tokens) directly to the LLM, it eliminates search overhead but incurs extreme total latencies (p50: 9.870s, p95: 17.117s). Similarly, the OpenAI implementation does not perform memory search, as it processes manually extracted memories from their playground. While this approach achieves impressive response generation times (p50: 0.466s, p95: 0.889s), it requires pre-extraction of relevant context, which is not reflected in the reported metrics.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2\">Our proposed <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.1\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;approach achieves the lowest search latency among all methods (p50: 0.148s, p95: 0.200s) as illustrated in Figure <a class=\"ltx_ref\" href=\"https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413v1#S4.F4.sf1\" title=\"Figure 4(a) ‣ Figure 4 ‣ 4.2 Cross-Category Analysis ‣ 4 Evaluation Results, Analysis and Discussion. ‣ Mem0: Building Production-Ready AI Agents with Scalable Long-Term Memory\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_ref_tag\">4(a)</span></a>. This efficiency stems from our selective memory retrieval mechanism and infra improvements that dynamically identifies and retrieves only the most salient information rather than fixed-size chunks. Consequently, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.2\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;maintains the lowest total median latency (0.708s) with remarkably contained p95 values (1.440s), making it particularly suitable for latency-sensitive applications such as interactive AI agents. The graph-enhanced <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;variant introduces additional relational modeling capabilities at a moderate latency cost, with search times (0.476s) still outperforming all existing memory solutions and baselines.\nDespite this increase, <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS4.p2.2.m2.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;maintains competitive total latencies (p50: 1.091s, p95: 2.590s) while achieving the highest J&nbsp;score (68.44%) across all methods—trailing only the computationally prohibitive full-context approach.\nThis performance profile demonstrates our methods’ ability to balance response quality and computational efficiency, offering a compelling solution for production AI agents where both factors are critical constraints.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_subsection\" id=\"S4.SS5\">\n<h3 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_subsection\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection\">4.5 </span>Memory System Overhead: Token Analysis and Construction Time</h3><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1\">We measure the average token budget required to materialise each system’s long<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.1\">-</span>term memory store.\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.2\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;encodes complete dialogue turns in a natural language representation and therefore occupies only <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.3\">7k</span> tokens per conversation on an average. Where as <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>&nbsp;roughly doubles the footprint to <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.4\">14k</span> tokens, due to the introduction of graph memories which includes nodes and corresponding relationships. In stark contrast, Zep’s memory graph consumes in excess of <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.5\">600k</span> tokens. The inflation arises from Zep’s design choice to cache a full abstractive summary at every node while also storing facts on the connecting edges, leading to extensive redundancy across the graph. For perspective, supplying the <em class=\"ltx_emph ltx_font_italic\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.6\">entire</em> raw conversation context to the language model—without any memory abstraction—amounts to roughly <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_bold\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.7\">26k</span> tokens on average, 20 times less relative to Zep’s graph.\nBeyond token inefficiency, our experiments revealed significant operational bottlenecks with Zep. After adding memories to Zep’s system, we observed that immediate memory retrieval attempts often failed to answer our queries correctly. Interestingly, re-running identical searches after a delay of several hours yielded considerably better results. This latency suggests that Zep’s graph construction involves multiple asynchronous LLM calls and extensive background processing, making the memory system impractical for real-time applications. In contrast, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS5.p1.1.8\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;graph construction completes in under a minute even in worst-case scenarios, allowing users to immediately leverage newly added memories for query responses.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1\">These findings highlight that Zep not only replicates identical knowledge fragments across multiple nodes, but also introduces significant operational delays. Our architectures—<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.1\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;and <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>—preserve the same information at a fraction of the token cost and with substantially faster memory availability, offering a more memory<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S4.SS5.p2.1.2\">-</span>efficient and operationally responsive representation.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S5\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">5 </span>Conclusion and Future Work</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S5.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S5.p1.3\">We have introduced <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S5.p1.3.1\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;and <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S5.p1.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>, two complementary memory architectures that overcome the intrinsic limitations of fixed context windows in LLMs. By dynamically extracting, consolidating, and retrieving compact memory representations, <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S5.p1.3.2\">Mem0</span>&nbsp;achieves state-of-the-art performance across single<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S5.p1.3.3\">-</span>hop and multi<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S5.p1.3.4\">-</span>hop reasoning, while <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1\"><semantics id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1a\"><msup id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1b\"><apply id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S5.p1.2.m2.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>’s graph<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S5.p1.3.5\">-</span>based extensions unlock significant gains in temporal and open<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S5.p1.3.6\">-</span>domain tasks. On the <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S5.p1.3.7\">LOCOMO</span> benchmark, our methods deliver 5%, 11%, and 7% relative improvements in single-hop, temporal, and multi-hop reasoning question types over best performing methods in respective question type and reduce p95 latency by over 91% compared to full<span class=\"ltx_text\" id=\"S5.p1.3.8\">-</span>context baselines—demonstrating a powerful balance between precision and responsiveness. <span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"S5.p1.3.9\">Mem0</span>’s dense memory pipeline excels at rapid retrieval for straightforward queries, minimizing token usage and computational overhead. In contrast, <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1\"><semantics id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1a\"><msup id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.2\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1b\"><apply id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S5.p1.3.m3.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>’s structured graph representations provide nuanced relational clarity, enabling complex event sequencing and rich context integration without sacrificing practical efficiency. Together, they form a versatile memory toolkit that adapts to diverse conversational demands while remaining deployable at scale.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S5.p2\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S5.p2.1\">Future research directions include optimizing graph operations to reduce the latency overhead in <math alttext=\"\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}\" class=\"ltx_Math\" display=\"inline\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1\"><semantics id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1a\"><msup id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\">Mem0</mtext><mi id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3\" mathsize=\"71%\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\">g</mi></msup><annotation-xml encoding=\"MathML-Content\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1b\"><apply id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1\"><csymbol cd=\"ambiguous\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.1.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1\">superscript</csymbol><ci id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2a.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\"><mtext class=\"ltx_mathvariant_monospace\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.2\">Mem0</mtext></ci><ci id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3.cmml\" xref=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1.1.3\">𝑔</ci></apply></annotation-xml><annotation encoding=\"application/x-tex\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1c\">\\texttt{Mem0}^{\\tiny g}</annotation><annotation encoding=\"application/x-llamapun\" id=\"S5.p2.1.m1.1d\">Mem0 start_POSTSUPERSCRIPT italic_g end_POSTSUPERSCRIPT</annotation></semantics></math>, exploring hierarchical memory architectures that blend efficiency with relational representation, and developing more sophisticated memory consolidation mechanisms inspired by human cognitive processes. Additionally, extending our memory frameworks to domains beyond conversational scenarios, such as procedural reasoning and multimodal interactions, would further validate their broader applicability. By addressing the fundamental limitations of fixed context windows, our work represents a significant advancement toward conversational AI systems capable of maintaining coherent, contextually rich interactions over extended periods, much like their human counterparts.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_section\" id=\"S6\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_section\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_section\">6 </span>Acknowledgments</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"S6.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"S6.p1.1\">We would like to express our sincere gratitude to Harsh Agarwal, Shyamal Anadkat, Prithvijit Chattopadhyay, Siddesh Choudhary, Rishabh Jain, and Vaibhav Pandey for their invaluable insights and thorough reviews of early drafts. Template for LLM as a Judge\n\n<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p3.2.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">Your task is to label an answer to a question as \"CORRECT\" or \"WRONG\".\nYou will be given the following data:\n(1) a question (posed by one user to another user),\n(2) a ‘gold’ (ground truth) answer,\n(3) a generated answer\nwhich you will score as CORRECT/WRONG.</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p4\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" based on their prior conversations.\nThe gold answer will usually be a concise and short answer that includes the referenced topic, for example:</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p5\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p5.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" related questions, the gold answer will be a specific date, month, year, etc. The generated answer might be much longer or use relative time references (like ‘last Tuesday’ or ‘next month’), but you should be generous with your grading - as long as it refers to the same date or time period as the gold answer, it should be counted as CORRECT. Even if the format differs (e.g., ‘May 7th’ vs ‘7 May’), consider it CORRECT if it’s the same date.</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p17.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">You have access to memories from two speakers in a conversation. These memories contain timestamped information that may be relevant to answering the question.</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p18\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p18.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p18.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\"># INSTRUCTIONS:</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p19\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p19.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p19.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">1. Carefully analyze all provided memories from both speakers</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p20\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p20.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p20.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">2. Pay special attention to the timestamps to determine the answer</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p21\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p21.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p21.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">3. If the question asks about a specific event or fact, look for direct evidence in the memories</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p22\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p22.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p22.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">4. If the memories contain contradictory information, prioritize the most recent memory</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p23\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p23.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p23.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">5. If there is a question about time references (like \"last year\", \"two months ago\", etc.),\ncalculate the actual date based on the memory timestamp. For example, if a memory from\n4 May 2022 mentions \"went to India last year,\" then the trip occurred in 2021.</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p24\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p24.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p24.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">6. Always convert relative time references to specific dates, months, or years. For example,\nconvert \"last year\" to \"2022\" or \"two months ago\" to \"March 2023\" based on the memory\ntimestamp. Ignore the reference while answering the question.</span></p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A1.p25\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A1.p25.1\"><span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter\" id=\"A1.p25.1.1\" style=\"font-size:80%;\">7. element</button>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_appendix\" id=\"A3\">\n<h2 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_appendix\">\n<span class=\"ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix\">Appendix C </span>Selected Baselines</h2><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px1\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">LoCoMo</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1\">The LoCoMo framework implements a sophisticated memory pipeline that enables LLM agents to maintain coherent, long-term conversations. At its core, the system divides memory into short-term and long-term components. After each conversation session, agents generate summaries (stored as short-term memory) that distill key information from that interaction. Simultaneously, individual conversation turns are transformed into ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1\">observations</span>’ - factual statements about each speaker’s persona and life events that are stored in long-term memory with references to the specific dialog turns that produced them. When generating new responses, agents leverage both the most recent session summary and selectively retrieve relevant observations from their long-term memory. This dual-memory approach is further enhanced by incorporating a temporal event graph that tracks causally connected life events occurring between conversation sessions. By conditioning responses on retrieved memories, current conversation context, persona information, and intervening life events, the system enables agents to maintain consistent personalities and recall important details across conversations spanning hundreds of turns and dozens of sessions.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px2\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">ReadAgent</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1\">ReadAgent addresses the fundamental limitations of LLMs by emulating how humans process lengthy texts through a sophisticated three-stage pipeline. First, in Episode Pagination, the system intelligently segments text at natural cognitive boundaries rather than arbitrary cutoffs. Next, during Memory Gisting, it distills each segment into concise summaries that preserve essential meaning while drastically reducing token count—similar to how human memory retains the substance of information without verbatim recall. Finally, when tasked with answering questions, the Interactive Lookup mechanism examines these gists and strategically retrieves only the most relevant original text segments for detailed processing. This human-inspired approach enables LLMs to effectively manage documents up to 20 times longer than their normal context windows. By balancing global understanding through gists with selective attention to details, ReadAgent achieves both computational efficiency and improved comprehension, demonstrating that mimicking human cognitive processes can significantly enhance AI text processing capabilities.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px3\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">MemoryBank</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1\">The MemoryBank system enhances LLMs with long-term memory through a sophisticated three-part pipeline. At its core, the Memory Storage component warehouses detailed conversation logs, hierarchical event summaries, and evolving user personality profiles. When a new interaction occurs, the Memory Retrieval mechanism employs a dual-tower dense retrieval model to extract contextually relevant past information. The Memory Updating component, provides a human-like forgetting mechanism where memories strengthen when recalled and naturally decay over time if unused. This comprehensive approach enables AI companions to recall pertinent information, maintain contextual awareness across extended interactions, and develop increasingly accurate user portraits, resulting in more personalized and natural long-term conversations.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px4\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">MemGPT</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.1\">The MemGPT system introduces an operating system-inspired approach to overcome the context window limitations inherent in LLMs. At its core, MemGPT employs a sophisticated memory management pipeline consisting of three key components: a hierarchical memory system, self-directed memory operations, and an event-based control flow mechanism. The system divides available memory into ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.1\">main context</span>’ (analogous to RAM in traditional operating systems) and ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.2\">external context</span>’ (analogous to disk storage). The main context—which is bound by the LLM’s context window—contains system instructions, recent conversation history, and working memory that can be modified by the model. The external context stores unlimited information outside the model’s immediate context window, including complete conversation histories and archival data. When the LLM needs information not present in main context, it can initiate function calls to search, retrieve, or modify content across these memory tiers, effectively ‘<span class=\"ltx_text ltx_font_italic\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.3\">paging</span>’ relevant information in and out of its limited context window. This OS-inspired architecture enables MemGPT to maintain conversational coherence over extended interactions, manage documents that exceed standard context limits, and perform multi-hop information retrieval tasks—all while operating with fixed-context models. The system’s ability to intelligently manage its own memory resources provides the illusion of infinite context, significantly extending what’s possible with current LLM technology.</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section class=\"ltx_paragraph\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px5\">\n<h4 class=\"ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph\">A-Mem</h4><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n<div class=\"ltx_para\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px5.p1\">\n<p class=\"ltx_p\" id=\"A3.SS0.SSS0.Px5.p1.1\">The A-Mem model introduces an agentic memory system designed for LLM agents. This system dynamically structures and evolves memories through interconnected notes. Each note captures interactions enriched with structured attributes like keywords, contextual descriptions, and tags generated by the LLM. Upon creating a new memory, A-MEM uses semantic embeddings to retrieve relevant existing notes, then employs an LLM-driven approach to establish meaningful links based on similarities and shared attributes. Crucially, the memory evolution mechanism updates existing notes dynamically, refining their contextual information and attributes whenever new relevant memories are integrated. Thus, memory structure continually evolves, allowing richer and contextually deeper connections among memories. Retrieval from memory is conducted through semantic similarity, providing relevant historical context during agent interactions</p><button class=\"sr-only button\" style=\"display: none;\">Report issue for preceding element</button>\n</div>\n<div class=\"ltx_pagination ltx_role_newpage\"></div>\n</section>\n</section>\n</article>\n</div>\n<footer class=\"ltx_page_footer\">\n<div class=\"ltx_page_logo\">Generated  on Mon Apr 28 01:35:44 2025 by <a class=\"ltx_LaTeXML_logo\" href=\"http://dlmf.nist.gov/LaTeXML/\"><span style=\"letter-spacing:-0.2em;
